VFP9 reference validation shows `THROW <expression>` should keep the expression in a generated base `Exception.UserValue`, and VFP9 `CATCH` supports `TO` and `WHEN` rather than a class-qualified catch head; do not implement the stale #3862 claims without new contrary VFP evidence. #3937 is closed as a stale duplicate of the shipped #3909 multi-config wrapper-output fix, and #3936 is closed after its named current-head preview/deleted-state regressions passed 13/13. #4124 still owns separate `*` fill and explicit sign picture work; `#4133` needs its RemoveObject/Destroy claim reconciled with VFP release semantics before any change there, and `#4126` needs a dedicated default-action/NODEFAULT design slice rather than a scattered flag check. Structural refactoring `#4127`, `#4128`, `#4129`, `#4130`, `#4168`, `#4169`, `#4170`, `#4171`, and `#4175` is shipped under `#2564`; broad refactor churn must stay separate from runtime behavior slices. `#4172`, `#4173`, `#4174`, `#4178`, `#4179`, `#4180`, and `#4181` are shipped narrow local-buffering children under `#3852`; modes 2 and 4 cover local pessimistic edits with session-scoped locks, while modes 3 and 5 now preserve original rows and reject non-forced optimistic updates after external changes before any batch write. Conflict arrays, remote/SQL semantics, OS-level interprocess locks, and pending-edit property changes remain separate. `#4090` is already closed upstream with exact-commit evidence. VFP9 reference media/source availability must be rechecked after remounts; `~/Downloads/FFC-master.zip` remains available for primary-source evidence.
- Durable CI note: keep required-check identity in the thin platform workflow wrappers, not in a reusable-workflow call chain that changes displayed job names. The wrappers own event triggers, platform runner, timeout, least-privilege permissions, immutable checkout, and concurrency keyed by platform plus event name plus PR/ref so a manual dispatch cannot cancel a push or pull-request run. The local composite action owns only shared setup/configure/build/full-CTest commands. Keep configure, bounded `--parallel 2` compilation, and CTest on the same runner; never upload, download, or reuse a CMake build tree as a release artifact. Native release readiness must require Linux, macOS, and Windows, while focused product workflows retain their own identities and intended product/test artifacts.
- Durable test-sharding note: every standalone executable target must retain an explicit main, even when the same topic shard is also compiled into an aggregate target. Shared test-support headers must use an include guard or `#pragma once`; Windows compilation exposes duplicate non-inline helper definitions that Linux may not compile because the guarded path is platform-specific. `#4198` further splits the control-flow routine-call regression into seven behavior-owned sources (153-803 lines) while preserving the aggregate target and invocation order; keep future routine-call coverage on the same sub-1k ownership pattern where practicable.
- Durable package note: resolve a relative PJX `HOMEDIR` from the project/document directory before testing whether it is a usable directory. Never let process CWD select a same-named relative home or enter generated working-directory/source-root metadata. Accept slash and backslash spellings, fall back to the PJX directory when the resolved home is missing or not a directory, and preserve existing absolute, current-drive-rooted, Windows drive-designator, and file-valued behavior.
- Durable DBF note: raw descriptor collision identity is the trimmed byte string Copperfin actually serializes, truncated to the current 11-byte descriptor span and folded only for ASCII case. Keep validation and serialization on the same helper and reject collisions before any staged write. This is corruption prevention, not VFP9 long-field-name support: `#4034` owns the documented 10-byte free-table limit and DBC logical-to-physical mapping.
- Durable package note: `output_root/<sanitized-project>` is generator-owned and must be replaced as one logical package; caller-owned siblings under `output_root` are not. Repeated materialization uses same-parent `<package>.copperfin-previous` as last-known-good recovery state, `<package>.copperfin-previous.owner` as its ownership proof, and `<package>.copperfin-materializing` as the interruption marker published before a fresh package tree is created. Owner/marker metadata is written to a unique same-directory temp file and renamed into place; only the authoritative names are read during recovery. Marker and owner must be direct regular files containing the exact versioned package identity, and the backup must be a direct directory, before reserved sibling paths are treated as Copperfin-owned; unrecognized, partial-temp, or symlink collisions are not followed or deleted. Normal failures remove the partial tree and marker, restore the backup first, then remove owner metadata; restart recovery trusts an owned backup over an owned-marker partial tree, while a complete unmarked package wins over an owned stale cleanup backup.
- Durable package note: resolve a selected PJX startup primary exact-first, then accept case-insensitive recovery only when every path component has one unique match. Preserve the matched directory-entry spelling in the package-relative staged path and both runtime/debug startup identities. Ambiguous or missing selected primaries and missing, ambiguous, or failed selected xAsset sidecars are package failures, never optional-asset warnings; route human failures through localization catalogs while keeping machine-readable package contracts invariant.
- Durable package note: for security-enabled package materialization, validate trust properties of the caller-supplied runtime-host source path before canonicalization. In particular, require `is_absolute()` on the original path before `weakly_canonical(...)`; otherwise an existing relative path becomes CWD-dependent trusted input. Canonicalization may normalize an already absolute path, but it must not manufacture the property being validated.
- Durable package note: packaged `startup_source` and executable payload paths must preserve their recorded package-relative identity. Prefer the current manifest-root rebind over any still-existing recorded build-root path, never restore basename-only or process-CWD fallback for executable content, reject strict relative escapes plus runtime-manifest absolute startup paths and startup-item roots outside the package, and normalize serialized Windows separators for rebinding and diagnostic basenames. Keep the separate `app.cfdebug` source-provenance contract able to execute its existing absolute source and working-directory paths, keep legacy runtime-manifest `startup_item` fallback package-relative and non-escaping, and preserve localized human diagnostics without localizing manifest keys, `status: error`, or exit code `4`.
- Durable package note: strict `app.cfmanifest` paths must reject every package-internal symlink, mount crossing, or Windows reparse component while allowing the package root itself to be reached through a deployment symlink/junction. Security verification and execution must consume the same direct-file byte snapshot for startup PRGs, verified includes, bridge source, xAsset primary/companion files, and generated bootstrap source; a matching `extension_payload` digest must never substitute for the copied-`asset` digest required by security startup. Every copied SCX/VCX/FRX/LBX/MNX asset must have its inferred memo sidecar admitted through the verified extension-payload set before `runtime.start`, with missing/undigested/redirected companions rejected through invariant security exit/audit contracts. Preserve logical package paths in debugger output, keep machine fields invariant, and do not apply these package restrictions to intentional external `app.cfdebug` source paths.
- Durable package note: when POSIX package content is held behind a pinned `/proc/self/fd/<n>` or `/dev/fd/<n>` root, enumerate staged archive files through descriptor-relative `openat`/`fstatat` traversal with symlink entries excluded. Do not use `std::filesystem::recursive_directory_iterator` on the fd-backed path or fall back to pathname traversal; APP payload assembly must retain declared assets, inferred FRX/FRT/SCX/SCT sidecars, and other staged files while preserving the existing containment and digest contracts.
- Durable package note: macOS may return `ELOOP` for `openat(..., O_NOFOLLOW)` on valid transaction files. For descriptor-backed manifest-pair reads, use the macOS-compatible open form only with `fstatat(..., AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` checks before and after the read plus opened-descriptor identity checks; keep the stricter `O_NOFOLLOW` path on Linux and preserve fail-closed symlink, non-regular, cross-device, and replacement-race behavior.
- Durable package note: public startup/debug source identity is the admitted logical spelling, not the canonical physical spelling used to verify and read bytes. Add deployment-logical or startup-namespace lookup keys only after strict containment, identity, and digest verification has produced the immutable snapshot they reference; never read or trust an alias independently. Preserve a rootless absolute manifest spelling only after strict admission succeeds, while generated/package-root paths continue to rebind into the deployment namespace. A package root itself may be reached through a deployment symlink/junction for startup and missing audit-output admission, but redirects beneath that trust root remain forbidden.
- Durable package note: schema-3 manifests use exactly one invariant `data_policy=package_writable`. Copied non-startup DBF assets are writable data, and their FPT/CDX/IDX/NDX/MDX companions must be completely declared through `data_payload=<path>|package_writable|<seed-sha256>` records. Seed digests are provenance, not immutable launch hashes; direct physical containment and complete membership remain mandatory on every security launch. Keep PRG/xAsset and DBC/DCT/DCX inputs immutable. Security-enabled `OPEN DATABASE` must consume the verified exact DBC/DCT/DCX byte snapshots already admitted by the manifest and must not execute stored procedures, triggers, validation rules, or database events without a separate trust-design slice.
- Durable package note: case-insensitive companion lookup must preserve the actual full directory-entry filename spelling in the staged destination and every manifest/debug path. Prefer an exact filename match, accept only one casefold fallback, and fail closed through `Runtime.Package.Error.AmbiguousCompanionPath` when multiple case-distinct entries match. Never let Windows' case-insensitive `exists()` response turn inferred lowercase SCT/VCT/MNT/FRT/LBT, DBF companion, or DCT/DCX names into the package identity.
- Durable runtime note: database lifecycle state is heap-backed and scoped by VFP data session. Preserve first-open `EXCLUSIVE` / `SHARED` and `NOUPDATE` settings when a DBC is reopened, keep `DBC()` limited to the current full path, keep `DBUSED(cDatabaseName)` limited to the documented current-data-session lookup, and do not add an undocumented `DATABASE()` alias.
- Durable runtime note: VFP string-plus and string-minus concatenation require character operands on both sides. Preserve ordinary `+` concatenation and `-` trailing-space relocation only for character pairs; mixed character/non-character operands must raise catalog-routed Error 107 prose, emit invariant `runtime.error` metadata, and remain continuable without recursive host-stack growth. Numeric `+` and supported Date/DateTime arithmetic retain their typed paths.
- Durable runtime note: VFP `SET EXACT ON` disregards trailing spaces only; leading spaces, tabs, NUL bytes, and non-space length differences remain significant. Apply the same rule to ordinary equality and shared function consumers such as `INLIST()`. `SET EXACT OFF` intentionally compares only until the end of the right operand and is therefore operand-order-sensitive; do not "symmetrize" it.
- Durable portability note: POSIX host-code-page fallback must parse locale values structurally, never by collecting arbitrary digits. Use the codeset component after `.` and before `@`; preserve `nl_langinfo` then `LC_ALL` / `LC_CTYPE` / `LANG` precedence, deterministic fallback 1252, Windows ACP/OEMCP behavior, and the separate VFP conversion-code-page allowlist.
- Durable portability note: Windows drive and UNC FULLPATH normalization must remain host-independent and root-locked, including extended UNC/drive forms. Preserve unsupported Windows device namespaces byte-for-byte; `\\.\\pipe` and `\\?\\GLOBALROOT` are not ordinary UNC shares and must not undergo hierarchical dot-segment reduction.
- Durable portability note: portable-core SQL/workspace/document token matching is byte-oriented and ASCII-only. Fold only ASCII letters, trim only ASCII whitespace, recognize Windows drive letters only in `A-Z` / `a-z`, and preserve every byte `>= 0x80` unchanged. Treat high bytes as identifier bytes for SQL boundaries so ASCII keywords cannot match inside UTF-8 tokens; this is deterministic byte preservation, not Unicode-aware case folding. Studio sidecar recovery owns the companion leaf name: prefer exact spelling, then return the actual case-folded directory entry rather than a synthesized lowercase extension.
- Durable runtime note: array parameter references are frame-local aliases to canonical caller storage, not copied arrays and not global entries keyed by callee parameter names; scalar references are live aliases rather than return-time-only copies. Preserve VFP9's call-mode distinction: `DO ... WITH` procedure identifiers remain reference-default, while UDF calls follow runtime-session-wide `SET UDFPARMS` with VALUE as the default, explicit `@` forcing reference, and parentheses forcing value. A bare array in VALUE mode contributes only element 1; nested scalar/array forwarding must retain ultimate caller storage identity, including `m.`-qualified forms. Keep this state independent between runtime sessions and shared across VFP data-session changes. `#4074`/`#4090` remove native-stack recursion from all UDF calls reached within `RETURN`; `#4068` owns the remaining non-`RETURN` expression contexts, and `#4071` owns the shipped array declaration scope/lifetime baseline.
- Durable runtime note: when a pending statement has a statement-specific cancellation contract, the outer iterative dispatcher must defer the cancellation flag to that handler. In particular, SLEEP must check cancellation before evaluating its duration and during the wait loop so localized prose, source metadata, transaction rollback, and machine events remain deterministic; non-SLEEP statements without a specialized path keep the generic async-cancel behavior.
- Durable portability note: runtime paths stored in narrow `std::string` buffers must use an explicitly narrow separator; do not pass `std::filesystem::path::preferred_separator` directly because its type is `wchar_t` under Windows. Every runtime translation unit that includes `windows.h` and uses the C++ standard-library `min` / `max` APIs must define `NOMINMAX` before that include so SDK macros cannot leak into qualified calls.
- Durable Windows stack note: keep the shared 16 MiB linker reserve on both the production Studio host and the large direct shared-model test executable until their oversized dispatch/test bodies are structurally decomposed under `#2564`. Apply the reserve through `copperfin_apply_windows_stack_reserve(...)` so MSVC and MinGW use one contract; do not treat the larger process reserve as permission to reintroduce recursive PRG execution or unbounded host-stack growth.
- Durable runtime note: ordinary `RELEASE <objectVar>` now removes the requested variable binding and releases the native object only when no tracked global, local, private, or array variable still contains that handle. Keep scope-exit cleanup and references held in native object properties/collections out of this helper until a dedicated lifetime graph design covers them; do not infer full VFP reference counting from the explicit-variable slice.
- Durable runtime note: native PRG class parsing records `PROTECTED` and `HIDDEN` property lists plus modifiers on class `FUNCTION`/`PROCEDURE` routines through `NativeMemberVisibility`. Instantiated objects retain effective visibility plus declaring-class metadata across their lineage, and `PEMSTATUS(object, member, 3)` reports protected/hidden members.
- Durable runtime note: native PRG visibility enforcement slice `#4155` rejects external reads, assignments, and direct method calls to protected/hidden members through the catalog-routed `Runtime.Prg.Core.Error.OleMemberAccessDenied` diagnostic. Same-class access and protected subclass access are allowed; explicit `DODEFAULT()` and indirect event-delegate dispatch remain separate follow-up scope under `#3850/#3217`.
- Durable runtime note: expression macro slice `#4156` performs textual `&macro` splicing inside the heap-backed parser so surrounding operators retain VFP precedence. Keep raw expression-text argument behavior for `EVAL()`, `EXECSCRIPT()`, `TEXTMERGE()`, and `LOOKUP()`, and keep command-target fallback plus nested macro indirection covered; do not restore isolated synchronous macro evaluation for ordinary expressions.
- Durable runtime note: scope-exit lifetime slice `#4157` releases object-valued locals and local-array entries before a PRG frame is popped, using the existing tracked-binding scan and native `Destroy` path. `THIS`, `PARENT`, `THISFORM`, and `THISFORMSET` aliases are contextual, global/private/array aliases and direct native collection-item entries keep objects alive, and a returned object remains live for the caller. `LOCAL` followed by `DIMENSION` must bind the array to the frame; do not extend this rule to full recursive native property/collection graphs or temporary expression values without a separate reference-graph design.
- Durable filesystem note: runtime pipeline translation units and generated native wrappers must carry paths through `copperfin::platform::path_to_utf8_string()` / `path_from_utf8_string()`, never `std::filesystem::path::string()` or `generic_string()` at a text boundary. Generated Windows wrappers must use wide module discovery and convert `u8string()` bytes explicitly; keep package-relative manifest/debug identities and localized display placeholders unchanged.
- Durable filesystem-path note: runtime and VFP asset code treats narrow strings carrying paths as UTF-8 transport values. Convert to `std::filesystem::path` only through `copperfin::platform::path_from_utf8_string`, convert back through `path_to_utf8_string`, and use wide Win32 file APIs where the C runtime has a narrow-path boundary. Keep package-content paths separate from source/debug paths, and preserve this rule for includes, sidecars, database/index readers, xAsset bootstrap aliases, diagnostics, and security hashing.
- Durable runtime-table note: `CREATE TABLE ... FROM ARRAY` consumes AFIELDS-compatible metadata rows through columns 1-4: name, type, length, and decimals. Accept the documented minimal four-column form and wider AFIELDS arrays, but keep field validation, free-table limits, DBF creation, command undo, and localized failure handling on the existing shared helpers; do not treat columns beyond decimals as writable schema directives.
- Durable runtime-table note: `COPY STRUCTURE EXTENDED` emits the documented 16-column metadata table: `FIELD_NAME`, `FIELD_TYPE`, `FIELD_LEN`, `FIELD_DEC`, `FIELD_NULL`, `FIELD_NOCP`, five field/table rule or error memo columns, `TABLE_NAME`, three trigger memo columns, and `TABLE_CMT`. Keep source field order and `FIELDS` filtering stable, route the output through the shared DBF writer, and leave metadata attributes blank or false when the current cursor model cannot truthfully provide them; do not invent validation/default/trigger semantics.
- Durable runtime-reflection note: documented VFP uses `PUTPEM()` for dynamic property/method writes. Keep `PUTPEM()` and the historical `SETPEM()` compatibility spelling on one shared dispatch path so native objects, Automation objects, accessor methods, read-only protections, list-control item selectors, localized warnings, and invariant machine contracts cannot drift between names.
- Durable path-test note: a VFP logical backslash path is not always a legal synthetic host filename. On Windows, exercise backslash behavior through a real nested native path; on POSIX, retain explicit backslash-bearing filename fixtures where the contract requires proving VFP-aware parsing. Do not make a cross-platform test attempt to create `E:\...` as one filename on Windows or reinterpret a real drive/UNC path as POSIX text.
- Durable portability note: `FULLPATH()` must classify Windows drive roots, UNC roots, and POSIX leading-slash roots independently of the current host filesystem. Preserve drive/UNC spellings, normalize VFP backslashes plus lexical dot segments in POSIX-rooted paths to portable forward-slash form, and use host `std::filesystem` relative resolution only after those cross-host absolute forms have been excluded.
- Durable runtime note: the trim-family baseline is intentionally space-only. `ALLTRIM()` now trims only `CHR(32)` in the ordinary runtime path, and the shared index-expression evaluator now applies the same space-only rule for `ALLTRIM` / `LTRIM` / `RTRIM` instead of using `std::isspace`; tabs, CR, LF, and other control whitespace remain significant unless a caller explicitly removes them by some other means.
- Durable runtime note: keep the native list-control single-cursor `Requery()` SQL/query-file path treating positive numeric `ORDER BY` tokens as projected-column ordinals when they fit the materialized row shape, and keep `SELECT DISTINCT` collapsing duplicate projected rows before refreshed list state is published. Preserve the existing row-order, `ListCount`, `DisplayValue`, selection, cursor-position, event, and stack-frugal frame-machine contracts while extending that mini-parser.
- Durable runtime note: `ADDPROPERTY()` and `REMOVEPROPERTY()` must treat class-defined native default properties the same way they already treat built-in identity/runtime-surface members for helper-based mutation. Keep `RuntimeOleObjectState::default_properties` as the snapshot of helper-protected class defaults, continue excluding the built-in child `parent` reference from that snapshot, and preserve ordinary direct assignment as the writable path for live property changes.
- Durable runtime note: host-driven/session-driven cancellation should now flow through the same transactional cleanup contract regardless of whether the cancel token is observed between statements, inside `SLEEP`, or during lock-retry waits. Preserve the shared cleanup behavior: roll back the active transaction journal, reset `TXNLEVEL()` to `0`, clear command-undo journals, emit the corresponding rollback/cancel events, and keep the public `PrgRuntimeSession::request_cancel()` hook narrow rather than turning it into a broader mutable execution-control surface without a specific issue driving that expansion.
- Durable runtime note: native event/member re-entrancy guards must stay exception-safe. Any path that inserts an `active_native_event_keys` entry for `object_handle:event_name` before invoking delegates, method bodies, or property accessors must release that key via RAII on every exit path, including propagated handler faults, so later explicit `RAISEEVENT()`, direct method dispatch, and property-read/property-write delegate dispatch are never silently disabled by a leaked active key.
- Durable runtime note: keep `INSERT INTO ... SELECT` on the shared query-materialization path rather than growing a second SQL evaluator. Materialize all source rows before mutating the target so self-insert commands remain finite, preserve selected/source cursor positions when those cursors are not also the mutation target, route target conversion through the existing `INSERT ... VALUES` field-write contract, and restore both persisted and in-memory target state when any row in the command fails.
- Durable runtime note: matched destination-field failures in `APPEND FROM ARRAY` must abort the command and flow through the command-undo rollback path; never treat a real DBF writer failure as a skippable column. After rollback, restore the cursor's record count, position, BOF/EOF/found flags, preserve the localized underlying writer diagnostic, and emit neither a success event nor an undo entry for the failed command.
- Durable runtime note: native DBF `APPEND FROM` may silently skip a source field only after descriptor matching proves that field is absent from the destination. A failed write to a matched field is a command failure and must preserve the localized writer diagnostic. Keep the shared file-based APPEND undo guard restoring cursor count, position, found, BOF/EOF, and active-order state whenever it rolls persisted files back.
- Durable runtime note: `IIF()` and `ICASE()` must remain lazy special forms, not ordinary eagerly evaluated functions. `IIF()` must evaluate the condition first and skip the untaken branch entirely; `ICASE()` must evaluate condition/result pairs in order, skip untaken result branches, treat `.NULL.` conditions as false, and return `.NULL.` when no condition matches and no otherwise result is supplied. Preserve those guarantees for guard idioms that would otherwise fault or trigger side effects on skipped branches.
- Durable runtime note: `EVL()` is now first-class and should continue using the runtime's `EMPTY()`-style emptiness rules, not `NVL()`'s null-only rule. Empty strings, zero numerics, false logicals, null/empty values, and typed empty values already representable in the runtime should all route to the fallback argument; non-empty values should preserve the original first argument.
- Durable runtime note: keep the first-pass `SET TEXTMERGE` contract session-scoped. Both bare `SET TEXTMERGE DELIMITERS` and terminal-`TO` `SET TEXTMERGE DELIMITERS TO` must reset to `<<` / `>>`; the latter is used by Microsoft-shipped `GENMENU.PRG` even though the help emphasizes the bare form. Single-argument delimiter updates should continue using the same token for both sides, `TEXTMERGE()` without explicit delimiter arguments should continue honoring the current session delimiters, plain `TEXT ... ENDTEXT` should continue honoring `SET TEXTMERGE ON`, and the explicit `TEXTMERGE` keyword should continue forcing merge evaluation even when `SET TEXTMERGE` itself is OFF.
- Durable runtime note: bare `SET FILTER TO` is the documented VFP filter-clear form and must remain equivalent to `SET FILTER OFF` for the selected or `IN`-targeted work area. Parse terminal `TO` through the shared SET-value helper; never require a trailing space after `TO`, store literal `TO` as the filter expression, localize invariant event details, or change machine-readable `runtime.filter: OFF` behavior.
- Durable runtime-host note: keep the native host tolerant of the Windows-style slash aliases that the generated launcher already accepts for direct invocation. `/debug` and `/locale <value>` should continue behaving exactly like `--debug` and `--locale <value>` during parsing and locale preselection, but the localized usage/help text should continue documenting the canonical `--` tokens only.
- Durable generated-launcher note: pass every runtime-host argument through `ProcessStartInfo.ArgumentList`; do not restore handwritten quoting or a flattened `Arguments` string. Insert invariant `--manifest` plus the selected manifest first, normalize only supported slash aliases, select `app.cfdebug` only for an actual debug request when the file exists, and otherwise retain `app.cfmanifest`. Keep the package root as the host working directory, contain `Process.Start` exceptions through the localized start-failure diagnostic and exit `5`, and emit console diagnostics as UTF-8. The generated project must compile `Program.cs` explicitly because its parent package directory is also the publish output; default SDK globs can exclude the source in that topology.
- Durable runtime-host note: packaged manifest verification must hash the staged sibling runtime host using the platform-correct bundled file name (`copperfin_runtime_host.exe` on Windows, `copperfin_runtime_host` elsewhere). Keep runtime-host tests deploying the executable under its actual built file name so non-Windows verification paths remain covered instead of masked by copied `.exe` aliases.
- Durable host note: default `license.cflicense` discovery in runtime, build, and inspect hosts must use the platform-resolved running executable path, never raw basename `argv[0]` or caller CWD. Keep explicit license overrides authoritative over `COPPERFIN_LICENSE_PATH`, and keep that environment path authoritative over the resolved executable sibling without changing invariant status fields, package metadata, or signature validation.
- Durable build-host note: default runtime-host sibling discovery must use the platform-resolved running build-host image, never raw basename `argv[0]` made absolute against caller CWD. Preserve `--runtime-host` over `COPPERFIN_RUNTIME_HOST_PATH` over deployed sibling fallback, and keep process regressions proving the exact staged bytes for every branch.
- Durable package-contract note: every rewrite of the final package-root `app.cfmanifest` / `app.cfdebug` pair, including late warning or finalization updates, must use `write_runtime_manifest_pair_atomically(...)`; never restore independent `write_text_file(...)` calls for those destinations. Keep both names as distinct direct package-root leaves, retain the pinned-directory/no-reparse/no-replace admission and versioned hash-journal recovery protocol, and preserve the rule that recovery either proves the complete new pair or restores the exact previous pair before cleaning owned artifacts. Fault-injection hooks belong only in `cf_runtime_pipeline_test`; production `cf_runtime_pipeline` must remain hook-free even when tests are enabled.
- Durable package-contract note: a Windows `X:tail` project asset uses that drive's current-directory semantics on Windows and remains unavailable on non-Windows hosts. Freeze an existing source to its absolute identity during planning so later parsing, companion discovery, and materialization cannot reinterpret mutable per-drive process state; never rebind a missing drive-relative source to its PJX-local `entry.name`. Remove the drive root from package-relative identity, require the content root to be exactly the direct `package_root/content` directory, and structurally and physically re-admit every primary and companion destination before writing. Preserve relative plan spelling, reject redirects, device crossings, multiply linked leaves, ADS and Windows device aliases, and keep unsafe optional source identities out of runtime/debug/AST/IR contracts. `#4066` owns handle-pinned inner writes and `#4063` owns the outer package-root transaction race.
- Durable package-contract note: keep `app.cfmanifest` focused on staged runtime execution data and keep source-oriented build/debug provenance in `app.cfdebug`. Runtime-host execution should continue depending on package-root/content-root/working-directory/startup fields plus the stable machine-readable execution contract, without reintroducing local source/build-plan paths into the runtime manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: `app.cfmanifest` audit paths are exact package-relative output identities, not permissive provenance paths. Validate physical containment before every append, reject redirects and multiply linked leaves, and publish an immutable audit-chain update through containment-preserving handles and same-directory atomic replacement. An invalid explicit path must fail closed without modifying any external identity or silently selecting the default leaf. Keep separately allowed external source/debug provenance confined to `app.cfdebug` behavior.
- Durable package-contract note: library-output materialization must continue staging the runtime host beside generated `DLL` / `FLL` / `OCX` wrappers under the package-planned platform-correct `copperfin_runtime_host[.exe]` filename before wrapper build/finalization. Generated launchers and generated wrappers should consume that same package-planned filename instead of re-hardcoding a Windows `.exe` suffix off-platform.
- Durable package-contract note: keep build-time artifact-path provenance out of `app.cfmanifest` too. `module_definition_path`, `library_api_manifest_path`, `fll_api_manifest_path`, `fxp_token_manifest_path`, and `app_archive_manifest_path` belong in `app.cfdebug` and the dedicated sidecar artifacts, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep `primary_output_path` out of `app.cfmanifest` too. The requested output path belongs in `app.cfdebug` and other inspection-oriented package surfaces; runtime-host execution should not depend on it being mirrored into the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep `primary_output_materialized` out of `app.cfmanifest` too. That flag is package-build/finalization state for inspection and should stay in `app.cfdebug`, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: for library outputs, keep export-source provenance (`library_function_source`) out of `app.cfmanifest` as well. That location data belongs in `app.cfdebug` and the dedicated DLL/FLL API manifests; the runtime execution manifest should stay limited to staged execution and non-source-sensitive library contract fields.
- Durable package-contract note: keep license inspection metadata out of `app.cfmanifest` too. `license_state`, `license_type`, `license_id`, `license_licensee`, `license_seats`, `license_subscription_expires`, and `license_perpetual_max_major_version` belong in `app.cfdebug` and the dedicated `--license-status` path, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep feature-flag inventory out of `app.cfmanifest` too. `feature_flag=...` lines are inspection-oriented rollout/build/debug/security metadata and should stay in `app.cfdebug`, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep compiler-contract digest inventory out of `app.cfmanifest` too. `compiler_contract=path|sha256` lines are build-artifact provenance for AST/IR/manifests/wrappers and should stay in `app.cfdebug`, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep export-symbol inventory out of `app.cfmanifest` too. `export_symbol=...` lines are callable-surface inspection data and should stay in `app.cfdebug`, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep `library_function_*` inventory out of `app.cfmanifest` too. Per-routine arity, kind, source, parameter, and call-surface lines are callable-surface inspection data and should stay in `app.cfdebug` plus the dedicated DLL/FLL `.api` artifacts, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep the `security_roles=` count summary out of `app.cfmanifest` too. That role-count summary is inspection-oriented security profile metadata and should stay in `app.cfdebug` and host preview/reporting surfaces, not the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep `launcher_mode=` and `launcher_fallback=` out of `app.cfmanifest` too. That launcher-selection summary is inspection-oriented host-selection metadata and should stay in `app.cfdebug`; runtime-host binding must continue working without those fields in the runtime execution manifest.
- Durable package-contract note: keep the `.NET` policy and gateway-decision mirror out of `app.cfmanifest` too. `dotnet_enabled`, allow/denylist summaries and items, parity summaries and items, and `dotnet_gateway_*` decision diagnostics are inspection-oriented extensibility policy metadata and should stay in `app.cfdebug`, while `dotnet_story` remains the only `.NET` runtime-manifest field that the runtime host still consumes for host-facing output.
- Durable package-contract note: keep the language/AI/extensibility mirror out of `app.cfmanifest` too. `language_integration*`, `ai_feature*`, `extensibility_guardrail*`, `language_integrations`, and `ai_features` are inspection-oriented extensibility inventory and should stay in `app.cfdebug`; `dotnet_story` remains the only host-facing extensibility field that the runtime manifest still carries.
- Durable test-contract note: when a focused test executes product binaries via `$<TARGET_FILE:...>` instead of linking their code directly, add explicit target dependencies for those binaries so narrow target rebuilds cannot run stale fixtures after shared-library or manifest-contract changes. Native Studio-host process-test executables inherit this through the end-of-file directory-target rule in `tests/CMakeLists.txt`; keep new process-host tests within that naming contract or add an equivalent explicit edge, and keep genuinely self-contained targets in the explicit exclusion list.
- Durable test-helper note: shared test suites should use `tests/test_environment_support.h` directly for environment reads and scoped set/restore behavior instead of introducing fresh pass-through wrappers in suite-local support layers. Add a local alias only when a suite genuinely needs a different contract, not just a renamed forwarder.
- Durable test-helper note: `ScopedDefaultLocaleCatalogEnvironment` forces `en-US` but must preserve a caller-provided catalog root when that root contains an openable regular `en-US/strings.json`; only missing or unusable roots should fall back to ancestor discovery. Keep override-preservation fixtures outside the fallback ancestor tree and restore both locale variables exactly after every scope.
- Durable test-helper note: tests intentionally exercising a shell-command contract must pass already argument-quoted command text through `copperfin::test_support::prepare_shell_command_for_system(...)`; `run_shell_command(...)` and captured `_popen`/`popen` paths retain that centralized Windows `cmd.exe /c` adaptation. Ordinary Studio-host fixture execution must instead use `run_process_capture(...)` with direct argv, native-width Windows paths, explicit working directory, and exact raw capture. Normalize line endings only through the opt-in Studio observation helper; never make raw process capture lossy.
- Durable release-validation note: the Windows deep workflow must retain enough job time for the complete all-target MSVC build, serial native CTest, VSIX build, standalone Studio build, designer build/smoke, and artifact upload. Keep the ceiling at 90 minutes unless measured workload changes justify another explicit issue; do not skip tests or weaken gates merely to fit the retired 45-minute limit.
- Durable runtime note: logical operators are now real parser-level semantics, not post-hoc text fragments. Keep `AND` / `.AND.` binding tighter than `OR` / `.OR.`, keep both operators short-circuiting so untaken right-hand operands are parsed but not executed, and keep the full logical-expression entrypoint wired through parenthesized expressions, function arguments, array selectors, and control-flow predicates instead of dropping those contexts back to comparison-only parsing.
- Durable runtime note: keyword unary negation is parser-level too. Keep `NOT` and `.NOT.` routed through the same unary-negation semantics as symbolic `!`, and preserve the ordering where unary keyword negation is recognized before any leading-dot member-access logic so `.NOT. (...)` cannot regress into an implicit OLE/member invocation fault.
- Durable runtime note: the VFP `$` containment operator is part of the comparison layer. Keep `cSubstring $ cWholeString` evaluating as substring-in-haystack boolean containment, not as a raw-left-operand passthrough, and preserve that behavior in ordinary assignments, parenthesized expressions, and control-flow predicates.
- Durable runtime note: the common VFP alternative inequality spellings `#` and `!=` must remain aliases for `<>` in the comparison parser. They should yield boolean inequality results, not degrade into raw-left-operand passthroughs, and they must keep working in ordinary assignments and control-flow predicates.
- Durable runtime note: the VFP exponentiation spellings `^` and `**` now live in a dedicated power tier under unary operators. Preserve representative precedence over multiplication and preserve unary-negation behavior by keeping exponentiation below the unary parser entrypoint and above the multiplicative layer.
- Durable runtime-host note: `RuntimeHost.Launch.Note.CompatibilityLauncher` is a user-facing fallback explanation for failed non-PRG xAsset bootstrap materialization, not a blanket statement that `SCX/VCX/FRX/MNX/LBX` startup families are unsupported. Keep machine-readable output keys stable, keep the detailed bootstrap error surfaced as a separate note, and localize any future wording changes through the shared catalogs.
- Durable runtime note: date-helper parsing must stay aligned with session date settings. Any helper that consumes ordinary runtime date/datetime text values should route through the `SET DATE` / `SET MARK` aware parse helpers rather than the legacy MDY-only parser, or DMY/YMD sessions will silently turn valid date values into `0` / empty results in adjacent helpers.
- Durable runtime note: `JUSTDRIVE()` must parse Windows path text independently of the host filesystem. Recognize a complete extended UNC root as namespace marker plus server plus share (`\\?\\UNC\\server\\share`), preserve the input spelling, and do not let the ordinary two-component UNC parser truncate it to `\\?\\UNC`. Accept only complete namespace UNC roots or explicit namespace `X:` drives; incomplete UNC, `GLOBALROOT`, and pipe namespace inputs have no drive. Keep ordinary drive/UNC, POSIX, relative, and empty contracts unchanged.
- Durable runtime note: `FORCEPATH()` must normalize the whole supplied directory before joining the source filename. Detect drive-letter and UNC directories as Windows style, single-leading-slash absolute directories as POSIX style, and mixed relative directories by their first separator; retain `/` for relative directories with no separator. Normalize separators only: do not collapse repeated separators, reduce dot segments, resolve roots, or consult the host filesystem.
- Durable security note: audit append must inspect an existing stream before opening it for append. Missing, zero-byte, and LF-empty-line-only streams may start at `GENESIS`; every nonempty record must be readable, newline-terminated, structurally five-field, and carry valid previous/observed hash shapes. Reject whitespace-mutated, malformed, or truncated tails without changing bytes. Keep full-chain cryptographic verification explicit in `verify_immutable_audit_chain(...)` rather than silently restarting or duplicating the verifier inside every append.
- Durable test-infrastructure note: `test_studio_host_json` now has built-in selection flags. Use `--list-tests` to enumerate registered names, `--filter <substring>` for a focused subset, and `--exact <name>` for one named test when validating narrow Studio-host JSON slices; keep the no-flag default running the full executable so existing CTest semantics stay intact. Porting the same pattern to other large native executables remains separate work under `#2562`.
- Durable test-infrastructure note: direct-process execution tests must use repository-built child fixtures when the launched process needs to accept arbitrary structured arguments. Do not use `/bin/true` / `/bin/false`, shell built-ins, or other host-specific commands as cross-platform `_spawnvp` / `execvp` fixtures; pass `$<TARGET_FILE:...>` through CMake and keep displayed-command assertions aligned with the product's platform quoting contract.
- Durable test-infrastructure note: assertions against serialized manifest/debug paths must pass host path strings through `quote_manifest_value(...)`. Windows serialization escapes each backslash, so concatenating raw `C:\...` text into `extension_payload=`, `data_asset=`, or `data_payload=` markers creates false platform failures even when the stored path identity is correct.
- Durable runtime note: shared `PrgValue` instances now carry explicit null state plus lightweight date/datetime string flavor metadata. Keep genuine `.NULL.` values distinguishable from uninitialized empties, keep string-backed date/time-producing functions and DBF `D`/`T` field paths tagged through ordinary copies/scatter/gather flows, and preserve `VARTYPE()` / `TYPE()` / `ISNULL()` semantics (`X` vs `U`, `D`, `T`) without widening this into a broad machine-contract change.
- Durable runtime note: `SET PROCEDURE TO <file> [ADDITIVE]` now has first-pass real helper-program semantics rather than dead SET-state text. Registered helper `.prg` files resolve through the active default directory with caller-file fallback, their routines are available to ordinary unqualified expression-level function/procedure calls plus unqualified `DO` / `CALL` / `SPAWN` routine dispatch after the caller program's own routines, `ADDITIVE` preserves first-opened-wins duplicate routine precedence, and non-additive `SET PROCEDURE TO` replaces the active helper list. Keep this on the existing iterative frame machine; do not reintroduce recursive helper dispatch.
- Durable runtime note: positional `APPEND FROM` source formats must preserve the typed order of an explicit `FIELDS a, b, c` list. For `TYPE SDF` and `TYPE DELIMITED` / `TYPE TAB` imports, including the selected SQL/result cursor DELIMITED path, treat source column N as the Nth explicitly listed field rather than the Nth field in destination-table declaration order; keep `FIELDS LIKE` / `FIELDS EXCEPT` on ordinary table-order filtering because they are pattern filters, not explicit reorderings.
- Durable runtime note: `ACOPY()` must not resize an already-existing destination array. Preserve the existing auto-create behavior for a genuinely missing target array, but when the target already exists and the requested copy would overflow it, clamp the copied element count to the target's current capacity and leave the target's shape unchanged.
- Durable runtime note: native `PageFrame.ActivePage` is now a first-pass bounded selector on top of the shipped `Pages` / `PageCount` lane. Keep it writable at runtime, keep non-empty pageframes clamped to the 1-based live page range, keep empty pageframes at `0`, and keep `GETPEM()` / `SETPEM()` / `PEMSTATUS()` / `AMEMBERS()` coherent without allowing `ADDPROPERTY()` / `REMOVEPROPERTY()` to shadow or erase the built-in surface.
- Durable runtime note: native `PageFrame.PageCount` is now a first-pass writable built-in surface on top of the shipped `Pages` collection lane. Keep it bounded to `0..99`, keep growth materializing default trailing `PageN` children in-order for empty/base and derived pageframes, keep shrink removing truncated page subtrees from exported runtime state instead of only detaching them, keep the `Pages` collection plus bounded `ActivePage` selector synchronized after every mutation, and keep direct assignment plus `GETPEM()` / `SETPem()` / `PEMSTATUS()` / `AMEMBERS()` coherent without allowing `ADDPROPERTY()` / `REMOVEPROPERTY()` to shadow or erase the built-in surface.
- Durable runtime note: numeric `EMPTY()` semantics are exact, not fuzzy. For floating numeric runtime values, only `0.0` is empty; do not reintroduce epsilon-tolerance checks that classify tiny nonzero values as empty.
- Durable runtime note: `DELETE ALL` and `RECALL ALL` are whole-table scopes, not current-record aliases. Preserve explicit `ALL` scope through parse/dispatch and route it through the shared table-scan deletion/recall path for both local and selected SQL/result cursors instead of letting it fall into the bare-current-record mutation branch.
- Durable runtime note: implicit same-area `USE` replacement is a real close, including shared-lock cleanup. When a new cursor reuses an occupied work area, release the outgoing cursor's table/record lock ownership through the same close path before overwriting the slot so later `UNLOCK` calls and later runtime sessions do not inherit leaked shared locks from the replaced table.
- Durable report-layout note: when an object overlaps more than one section, the containing section is the one whose bounds contain the object's top edge, not simply the latest later band it overlaps. Keep both the shared layout snapshot helper and the section-geometry reflow helper using that same top-edge rule so tall objects retain stable `containingSectionId` / `sectionRelativeTop` metadata and move with the correct section during band-top edits.
- Durable runtime note: `CATCH TO` inside an active `ON ERROR` handler must materialize the fault that the inner `TRY/CATCH` just caught, not the outer handler snapshot. Keep Exception-object materialization on the live `last_error_*` metadata/compatibility payload for the current caught fault, while preserving the separate ON ERROR compatibility behavior where later top-level `MESSAGE()` / `AERROR()` inside the handler revert to the original outer fault after the inner catch completes.
- Durable runtime note: `TRIM()` is a trailing-space operation, not a left trim. Keep the shared PRG string-function dispatcher routing `TRIM()` with `RTRIM()` semantics while `LTRIM()` remains the separate leading-space helper, and preserve the space-only trim baseline rather than widening this back into `std::isspace` behavior.
- Durable runtime note: `RENAME <old> TO <new>` must fail closed when the destination path is already occupied. Keep the preflight destination-exists check ahead of the filesystem rename call, surface that path through the localized `Runtime.Prg.Dispatch.Error.RenameFileTargetExists` key, and preserve both source and destination bytes on the failure path instead of inheriting host-platform overwrite semantics.
- Durable runtime note: `STUFF()` now follows VFP's insert-only boundary rule when `nStartReplace <= 0`. In that front-of-string boundary case, ignore `nCharsReplace` completely and insert `cReplacement` at the start; keep the existing replacement-length clamp behavior only for positive start positions.
- Durable DBF note: literal `"null"` text is valid data for direct string-bearing DBF fields. Keep `C`, `V`, and `Q` field writes treating `"null"` / `"NULL"` as ordinary text during create/replace flows; do not reintroduce blanket null-token sniffing on those field types unless the storage contract grows an explicit nullability channel.
- Durable DBF note: memo-backed table parsing and `PACK MEMO` now distinguish successful empty decoded payloads from unresolved memo reads. Empty-decoding `M/G/P` payloads should remain empty in `display_value`, and any memo repack path must preserve raw sidecar bytes rather than rebuilding from localized or filtered display text; if a referenced payload cannot be recovered, fail closed instead of synthesizing replacement content.
- Durable runtime note: the shared `TRANSFORM()` baseline now includes representative `@R` literal-character templates plus comma-only numeric grouping without a decimal point. Treat the `@R` separator space as syntax, not output, and keep grouped integer pictures flowing through the same `SET POINT` / `SET SEPARATOR` / `SET CURRENCY` symbol path as decimal numeric pictures.
- Durable Studio-host note: builder/editor-action/designer/toolbox child launch execution should stay argv-based, not shell-based. Keep `build_shell_command()` as display/diagnostic text only; do not route it back through `std::system()` or reintroduce shell parsing as the execution contract for child launch commands.
- Durable report-layout note: live objects that still belong to deleted sections remain live for preview-bounds purposes. Preserve deleted-section membership and deleted-section object ordering for those objects, but keep their geometry in `preview_bounds_*`; `deleted_preview_bounds_*` is for genuinely deleted bands/objects only.
- Durable report-layout note: an already-equal supported FRX/LBX property write must remain a true no-op. Preserve both primary asset and memo-sidecar bytes, identity/provenance fields, and stable host JSON while retaining the normal changed-value transaction and undo path; mounted `invoice.frx` / `cust.lbx` `GRIDV` coverage under `#4253` proves this boundary.
- Durable visual-asset note: undo journals are versionless native local state. Bound every length/count/index conversion by the bytes that actually remain plus platform/container limits before allocating, accept the pre-grouped scalar form only at exact EOF, and reject partial count fields or trailing bytes. Do not introduce arbitrary content-size ceilings that make previously valid large journals unreadable. Preflight every target in a grouped entry before applying the first change so corrupt later selectors cannot leave partial target mutations.
- Durable report-settings note: mounted installed VFP9 `invoice.frx` / `cust.lbx` now treat missing root `LEFTMARGIN` and `RIGHTMARGIN` as memo-backed settings-record `EXPR` lines, not as direct fields. Keep the shared host and shared WinForms editor aligned on that contract: both properties should materialize through their invariant names with `fieldIndex = 6` / `sourceLineIndex = 3` provenance on those mounted samples rather than being hidden behind snapshot-presence gating.
- Durable runtime note: `CONTINUE` now follows VFP's locate-resume semantics again; it is no longer accepted as a loop-control synonym. Use `LOOP` for `FOR` / `DO WHILE` / `SCAN` continuation, and use `CONTINUE` only to resume the active `LOCATE FOR` search on the current work area.
- Durable runtime note: the shipped native list-control baseline now includes single-column `AddItem()`, representative multicolumn `AddItem(..., nIndex, nColumn)` follow-through, builtin `Clear()` with the mounted VFP9 `RowSourceType = 0` gate, read-only `ListCount`, representative writable `List(nIndex)` / `List(nIndex, 1)` plus representative writable multicolumn `List(nIndex, nColumn)` and `List(NewIndex, nColumn)` row-churn follow-through, representative writable `ListItem(nItemID)` / `ListItem[nItemID]` plus multicolumn item-addressed writes keyed by stable item ID rather than display order, `RemoveItem()`, representative `RemoveListItem(nItemID)`, read-only `NewIndex`, read-only `NewItemId`, representative multicolumn `AddListItem()`, writable `ListItemID` selection coherence, representative `ListBox.Selected(nIndex)` row-selection coherence, representative `ListBox.MultiSelect` selection-mode coherence, representative `SelectedID(nItemID)` item-id selection coherence, representative `Sorted` row-order maintenance, first-pass selected `Value` coherence driven by `BoundColumn`, first-pass builtin `BoundTo` plus builtin `ListBox.BoundColumn` / `ListBox.ColumnCount` / `ListBox.ColumnWidths` reflection, first-pass builtin `ListBox.ControlSource` reflection, first-pass `BoundTo = .F.` source-type selection semantics keyed from the bound `ControlSource` value kind when available, first-pass direct-selection and `Requery()` write-back through bound memory-variable and cursor-field `ControlSource` targets, and first-pass `Requery()` refresh for value-list, array, alias, representative single-cursor fields, and representative single-cursor SQL/query-file row sources over the same-session runtime row state for native `ComboBox`/`ListBox` controls, including representative single-cursor and joined query refresh through bare source designators plus representative SQL-style source aliases, bare `JOIN`, explicit `INNER JOIN`, `LEFT JOIN`, and `LEFT OUTER JOIN`. It is still intentionally first-pass: broader joins/full-SQL query parity, broader list-control binding refresh parity, and non-direct write-back edge cases are not claimed complete, so this remains a focused common-in-the-wild runtime baseline rather than a blanket claim of every VFP list-control edge case.
- Durable runtime note: ordinary native PRG self-calls now late-bind correctly from inherited method bodies. `THIS.Method()` and bare self-calls made on the live current object resolve from the object's most-derived runtime class, including across external-base PRG inheritance, while `DODEFAULT()` remains the explicit base-relative dispatch path that starts above the currently executing method's defining class.
- Durable runtime note: same-session native PRG objects now carry first-pass `ReadExpression()` parity for live property sources. Preserve class-body property expression text, keep direct runtime assignment reads on the stack-frugal frame path when source expression text is available, keep reflective method discovery coherent through `GETPEM()` / `PEMSTATUS()` / `AMEMBERS()`, and treat missing properties deterministically as empty-string reads instead of fabricating method bodies or mutating machine-readable runtime identifiers.
- Durable runtime note: VFP help explicitly documents the `OLERequestPendingTimeout = 5000` and `OLEServerBusyRaiseError = .F.` defaults, but the `OLEServerBusyTimeout` topic documents behavior without an explicit numeric default. Copperfin currently seeds a representative `OLEServerBusyTimeout = 5000` value on native OleControl hosts to keep timeout-policy inspection/programmatic-control paths deterministic until a stricter VFP-default citation is available.
- Validation note: on Linux, the full `mono vsix/Copperfin.DesignerSmokeTests/bin/Release/net472/Copperfin.DesignerSmokeTests.exe` run is still not a clean release gate because the unmanaged Mono/libgdiplus tail remains unstable and the suite still lacks targeted filtering, but the earlier managed-assembly packaging gap is now closed. The `net472` output folder now carries `System.Memory.dll`, `System.Text.Json.dll`, and the rest of the resolved managed dependency set, so offscreen Mono probes can progress into existing behavioral assertions instead of aborting on later loader faults. `#3824` used `git diff --check`, `dotnet build vsix/Copperfin.DesignerSmokeTests/Copperfin.DesignerSmokeTests.csproj -c Release`, populated-output inspection, and an offscreen Mono probe that now reaches the existing report-layout shell-summary failures after the earlier localized/project-workspace coverage. Treat Linux Mono smoke as advisory coverage for these managed slices until the remaining assertion noise and filtering gap are closed. Earlier focused validation remains as described below for the shared designer/runtime workflow families.
- Durable report-settings note: the shared managed deleted-settings seam now explicitly carries `BOTMARGIN` alongside `TOPMARGIN`, left/right margins, and the memo-backed printer/page-setup fields. Keep deleted report/label settings selection, update, localization, and machine-contract ordering coverage aligned on the invariant `BOTMARGIN` property name rather than relying on the separate host-only missing-bottom-margin seam.

Earlier apparent `ctest` failures for `test_prg_engine_control_flow` were caused by launching the same large native test binary concurrently through both direct execution and `ctest`, while many cases reuse fixed temp roots under the system temp directory; avoid treating concurrent duplicate invocations of the same native test executable as a meaningful validation signal.
- Validation note: the aggregate `test_studio_host` target is buildable again after `#3507` (`157da636`), so narrow Studio-host slices can use either that aggregate target or the split host executables as appropriate instead of assuming the old duplicate-`main` / unresolved-helper link failure is still present.
- Repository licensing changed: prior versions were GPL-3.0-only; new versions are dual-licensed (free individual/non-commercial use plus a paid Commercial License). See `LICENSE.md`, `MIGRATION_NOTICE.md`, and `LEGAL_FAQ.md`. A license-status mechanism (`cf_licensing`, `--license-status`, `app.cfmanifest`/`app.cfdebug` stamping, Studio JSON `licenseProfile`) was added in commits `d94b31f7`/`21177bd9`; it is display/audit-only and never gates functionality.
- `#3211` tracks two pre-existing bugs discovered while adding the licensing work above (unrelated to licensing itself, both confirmed pre-existing via A/B revert): the Windows `/sdl` test-helper `getenv` failure is now addressed in the touched core tests via a shared Windows-safe environment helper, and Windows builds now reserve a 16 MiB stack for `copperfin_studio_host` through target link settings. Root cause (see issue comments) and the 16 MiB fix are both independently verified: Debug and Release `copperfin_studio_host.exe` now both handle `--json` against a trivial `.scx` and the full `solution.pjx` sample with exit code 0, and `test_licensing_status` (22/22) still passes after Codex's `test_environment_support.h` refactor. The underlying oversized Studio host entrypoint (`apps/copperfin_studio_host/main.cpp`, ~4175 lines, ~150+ inlined property-setter blocks not yet split into `try_handle_*` helpers) still merits a later structural refactor as the real fix; the stack reservation is a verified-effective mitigation, not that refactor.
- PRG-level call-stack frugality (a founding requirement) was investigated in depth per user request: `DO`/`CALL`/`DO FORM` are confirmed genuinely frugal -- an explicit heap-backed frame stack inside an iterative `run()` loop (`src/runtime/prg_engine.cpp`), with a tested, configurable `max_call_depth` guardrail (default 1024, `prg_engine_session.inl:1652`) that raises a catchable error before any native stack risk. `#3216` is now covered through that same stack-frugal frame machine rather than a recursive side path, and `#3217` has been refreshed from a stale missing-feature report into the active parent lane for remaining native-OOP/runtime residuals. Treat that lane as common-in-the-wild VFP runtime-parity work, not a fringe wishlist item.
- Environment-access hardening parent `#3214` is closed. Preserve its shared Windows-safe variable/path helpers and open a new prompt-sized child under the live hardening lane only when fresh evidence identifies a remaining direct-access defect.
- E2/#23 remains open mostly for evidence-audit/closure cleanup. Do not open more E2 wrapper work unless new APIs or fresh regression evidence show a real implementation gap.
- Localization/release-readiness slices under `#2348`/`#113` have shipped through `#2352` and `#1856`; continue them only if live issue state makes them higher priority than E3/#24.
- Shipped localization slice `#4384` under `#2348`: native PRG record-lock timeout errors now use `Runtime.Prg.Records.Error.RecordLockTimeout` with `context` and `reprocess` placeholders in all four catalogs. The `runtime.lock_timeout` event detail remains invariant, and `test_prg_engine_table_mutation` covers en-US, es-419, pt-BR, qps-ploc, placeholder preservation, and retry behavior.
- Shipped follow-up localization slice `#4385` under `#2348`: implicit `APPEND BLANK` table-lock timeout errors now use `Runtime.Prg.Records.Error.TableLockTimeout` with `context` and `reprocess` placeholders in all four catalogs. Explicit FLOCK remains boolean-only, `runtime.lock_timeout` telemetry is invariant, and `test_prg_engine_table_mutation` covers two-data-session contention across en-US, es-419, pt-BR, and qps-ploc.
- Shipped follow-up localization slice `#4386` under `#2348`: shared-cursor table-maintenance failures now use `Runtime.Prg.Records.Error.RequiresExclusiveCursorForMaintenance` with the `command` placeholder in all four catalogs. `test_set_exclusive_controls_table_maintenance_guards` covers en-US, es-419, pt-BR, qps-ploc, pause behavior, and unchanged DBF bytes.
- Durable VSIX localization note: `InstalledProductRegistration` must use stable resource IDs rather than literal display strings. Keep the neutral `CommandResources.resx` and culture satellites aligned with `VSIX.Package.Name` and `VSIX.Package.Description` in the shared catalogs, including decorated qps-ploc values; package identity, version, command IDs, and machine-readable contracts remain invariant.
- Durable VSIX editor-registration note: every `ProvideEditorExtension` entry uses invariant `NameResourceID = 200`; keep that resource defined in the neutral and culture-specific `CommandResources*.resx` files and aligned with the shared `AssetEditor.Title` key. Retain the English `DefaultName` as the Visual Studio registration fallback, and do not change editor factory IDs, extensions, or machine contracts when adding locales.
- Durable PRG focus note: during a native `SetFocus()` transition, the previous control's `Valid` result and frame-scoped `NODEFAULT` request are separate signals. False/zero rejects validation; `NODEFAULT` suppresses only the modeled focus transition. Keep `NODEFAULT` captured from that specific iterative method frame, consume stale delegate state without letting it suppress the native default, and preserve the `Valid` -> `LostFocus` -> active-control -> `GotFocus` ordering for accepted transitions.
- Shell/UI backlog guidance is now captured in `#3050` (platform UI standards for native hosts), `#3051` (dockable Command and terminal surfaces, including the default bottom-tabbed VSIX Command window behavior), and `#2998` (future curated standalone extension support without loading the Copperfin VSIX). Those three issues already cover the current durable UI direction; reuse them rather than opening duplicate backlog items unless new scope appears beyond the existing host-shell, docking, terminal, extension-compatibility, or localization-ready shell-text boundaries.
- Durable VSIX UI note: `#2996` now has a shipped first-pass host-mode chrome compaction in the shared WinForms editor. Keep the Visual Studio document-host path visually compact by suppressing the standalone title/subtitle/guidance banner and using tighter outer padding there, while preserving the richer localized title chrome for standalone Studio mode where the control is the primary shell surface.
- Durable managed-launch note: project Build/Run/Debug and detached launcher creation must stay on the shared wrapper-aware `ProcessStartInfo` path. On Windows, configured `.cmd` / `.bat` hosts use `COMSPEC /d /c`; `.exe` and extensionless hosts stay direct, and non-Windows paths remain direct. Preserve explicit empty arguments, invariant CLI/output/environment contracts, and catalog-routed start failures with retained host diagnostics; do not let `Process.Start` exceptions escape into VSIX or standalone UI workflows.
- Validation note: `#2996` used `git diff --check`, `dotnet build vsix/Copperfin.DesignerSmokeTests/Copperfin.DesignerSmokeTests.csproj -c Release`, and direct Mono exact-smoke execution of `SmokeLocalizedAssetEditorChrome`, `SmokePseudoLocalizedAssetEditorChrome`, and `SmokeLocalizedHostModeChromeCompaction`. The focused managed smoke coverage now proves standalone-localized title chrome still appears when `EmbeddedStudioShell = true` and that the default Visual Studio host mode hides that standalone banner while keeping localized behavior intact. `dotnet build vsix/Copperfin.VisualStudio/Copperfin.VisualStudio.csproj -c Release` still fails on this Linux box with the pre-existing missing `Microsoft.VisualStudio.Validation` SDK-task dependency, so Windows/Visual Studio remains the required validation lane for full VSIX packaging.

## Required Reading

Read only what is needed for the chosen slice:

- `agents.md` for operating rules and safety traceability.
- `docs/23-phase-a-dependency-breakdown.md` for current lane status and historical dependency evidence.
- `docs/28-repository-ontology.md` when the slice needs current repo-structure grounding instead of aspirational architecture summaries.
- `docs/22-vfp-language-reference-coverage.md` when the slice touches VFP/runtime language coverage.
- `docs/safety/triage-rubric.md` for feedback triage.
- `docs/safety/test-coverage-gap-analysis.md` when adding tests.
- Runtime, designer, or host files directly relevant to the selected child issue.

`remaining-work.md` is intentionally deprecated as an active planning source. `issues.txt` is a dated local snapshot, not planning authority; verify live GitHub state before choosing work.

## Validation And Updates

- Run focused tests for the selected slice plus `git diff --check`.
- For safety-relevant documentation, keep `DQ-*`, `DV-*`, and `HZ-*` traceability linked to `docs/safety/hazard-register.md`.
- Before a release tag, run `scripts/validate-safety-traceability.ps1` or the Safety Traceability Gate workflow against the intended release issue set and archive the report.
- Safety evidence note (2026-07-21): `docs/safety/test-coverage-gap-analysis.md` was refreshed against current main. GAP-01 through GAP-09 now point to focused DBF/PRG/security/runtime-host regressions, but the document intentionally does not claim formal GCOV/MC/DC completeness. Before release tagging, still run the safety traceability validator against the intended closed issue set, archive its report, and review the hazard register for newly introduced hazards.
- Safety validator note (2026-07-21): `scripts/validate-safety-traceability.ps1` now distinguishes documentation traceability issues from implementation/test issues that merely carry the broad `safety` label. Select the intended documentation issue set for release validation; the validator recognizes the `documentation` label, a `[docs-safety]` title, or `DQ-*` identifiers in the body. The classifier regression is covered by `test_safety_traceability_workflow_contract`.
- Update `agent-handoff.md` only when the last shipped slice, current lane, or next action changes.
- Update `CHANGELOG.md` for lasting repo changes or material tracked-documentation changes.
- Shipped build-host validation slice `#3886` rejects invalid or empty `--configuration` values before plan creation. Accept only the invariant `debug` and `release` tokens after ASCII case/edge-whitespace normalization; preserve nonzero CLI failure, `status: error`, and catalog-backed prose across locales.
- Shipped sample-discovery hardening slice `#4193`: `tests/test_studio_host_real_sample_support.h` uses error-code directory-iterator increments and regular-file probes so an inaccessible or changing mounted media root, or an incomplete report/label memo pair, is treated as unavailable instead of throwing from CTest. `test_studio_host_real_sample_discovery` covers incomplete, complete, missing-sidecar, and regular-file overrides on portable native builds. Full native CTest and Windows mounted-media validation remain required before release closure.
- Shipped Windows validation slice `#4216`: `.github/workflows/windows-deep-validation.yml` always runs the designer smoke executable through `scripts/run-required-designer-smoke.ps1` and measures runtime-package, PRG-debugger, xAsset, report, and menu smoke stages. The wrapper fails on a nonzero executable result and reports external fixture-dependent `SKIP:` lines as warnings; `scripts/run-windows-deep-smoke.ps1` remains the strict gate for required VFP9 sample assets and selected native hosts. Windows execution remains required because this Linux host cannot run the native VFP9 sample/debug path.
- Shipped Windows packaging slice `#4215`: the Windows install tree now contains `bin/studio/Copperfin.Studio.exe` and its `.config` beside the native `bin/copperfin_studio_host.exe`. `CMakeLists.txt` keeps this managed install target Windows-only, and `tests/run_studio_install_contract_check.cmake` fails if the required files are missing or unexpected files are staged. The installer workflow builds the managed shell before CPack; retain the separate VSIX artifact and leave Linux/macOS install layouts unchanged. Hosted Windows clean-machine launch/install smoke remains required.
- Reopened #4217 after hosted Windows evidence showed that `tests/run_studio_install_contract_check.cmake` incorrectly expected `CMAKE_SIZEOF_VOID_P` in `CMakeCache.txt`. `CMakeLists.txt` now persists the configure-time result as the internal `COPPERFIN_NATIVE_POINTER_SIZE` cache value, and the verifier consumes that stable contract while retaining the x64 generator and managed-project checks. Keep the separate Windows x86 DECLARE workflow intact, and do not add this requirement to Linux/macOS packaging.
- Shipped packaging version slice `#4218`: `project(Copperfin VERSION ...)` now feeds CPack and generates `CopperfinPackageVersion.txt`; `tests/run_package_version_contract_check.cmake` validates both VSIX manifests and `InstalledProductRegistration` against that generated value. `tests/run_cpack_artifact_contract_check.cmake` derives exact platform artifact names from the same file, while uploads use only version-independent filename patterns after exact verification. Keep the version invariant across locales and preserve the intentional machine-readable artifact identifiers.
- Shipped standalone localization slice `#4219`: `CopperfinExternalLocaleCatalog` loads installed `share/copperfin/locales/<locale>/strings.json` catalogs before compiled managed fallback, with `COPPERFIN_UI_LOCALE` ahead of `COPPERFIN_LOCALE`, standalone `--locale` ahead of both, and `COPPERFIN_LOCALE_DIR` as the explicit root override. `tools/Copperfin.LocalizationCatalogGenerator` synchronizes all managed catalog keys into the four shared locale files, and `tests/run_managed_compile_check.cmake` runs its parity mode. Keep installed catalogs present for Windows Studio smoke and preserve all machine identifiers/contracts across locale changes.
- Active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: POSIX transaction markers use the pinned parent descriptor with atomic `openat`/`fsync`/`renameat` writes, and transaction callers preserve localized marker-write diagnostics instead of replacing them with generic start failures. Focused Linux build-host/runtime tests pass; hosted macOS run `29659100580` still failed with the prior generic symptom because that run predates the latest call-site preservation change. Inspect the next macOS run before changing package-directory/content-root handling, and preserve parent identity, rollback, containment, and machine contracts.
- Follow-up for active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: POSIX transaction setup now creates the direct package-root child through the pinned parent descriptor with `mkdirat`, and validates an existing child using no-follow `fstatat`; it no longer depends on high-level `/dev/fd` traversal for that boundary. Focused Linux build-host/runtime tests pass. Hosted macOS run `29659699880` confirmed the marker symptom was superseded by package-root creation, so inspect the next run for content-root/staging failures before widening the change.
- Follow-up for active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: POSIX materialization now skips the redundant high-level package-root `create_directories` call because transaction `begin()` already creates and owns that root through the pinned descriptor. Focused Linux build-host/runtime tests pass; the next hosted macOS run should identify the first remaining content-root or staging boundary.
- Follow-up for active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: transaction-backed POSIX content roots now create and verify the direct `content` child through the package descriptor with `openat`/`mkdirat`/no-follow `fstatat`; external content roots retain the existing physical-containment path. Focused Linux build-host/runtime tests pass; inspect the next macOS run for asset-staging traversal failures.
- Follow-up for active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: POSIX content-root validation now carries the direct `fstatat` result forward instead of re-inspecting the fd-backed child through high-level filesystem traversal. Focused Linux build-host/runtime tests pass; the next macOS run will confirm whether asset staging is the remaining boundary.
- Follow-up for active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: transaction-backed POSIX materialization now adopts the open package/content descriptors for `content_identity`; it no longer reopens the fd-backed content path by name. Focused Linux build-host/runtime tests pass; inspect the next macOS run for asset-staging failures and preserve this descriptor boundary.
- Follow-up for active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: fd-backed POSIX package/content roots now bypass only physical-path canonicalization, which cannot reliably describe `/dev/fd` names on macOS; parent identity, no-follow descriptor opens, `mkdirat`, `fstatat`, and descriptor ownership remain enforced. External roots retain the full physical-containment validator. Focused Linux tests pass; inspect the next macOS run for staging failures.
- Follow-up for active macOS package-materialization slice `#4221`: recognized fd-backed POSIX content roots now return directly after descriptor-relative relationship, creation, and no-follow directory checks; the unreachable duplicate fallback was removed. Runtime launcher inventory must apply the same conversion to every plan/digest path. Windows transaction-marker ownership uses `GetFileAttributesW` with directory/reparse rejection before reading the marker; focused Linux validation passes and the exact-head Windows rerun is the decisive evidence.
- Windows runtime package staging uses the pinned parent writer, but nested direct content directories must be prepared and physically validated before that writer opens the parent. Existing destination files must be checked for reparse/directory status and `nNumberOfLinks == 1` before `MoveFileExW`; post-move checks alone are too late for hard-link aliases. This is tracked by #4294.
